# Darth Krayt / A'Sharad Hett

- **Aliases:** Krayt, A'Sharad Hett, A’Sharad Hett, A'Sharad, Sharad Hett, Vong Krayt
- **Era:** Clone Wars–Legacy
- **Continuity:** default Legends; entries present for: Canon, Legends
- **Research tier:** MASTERY (Legacy-era version only); DEEP
- **Entries:** 2249 (1161 with quotations; 170 quotations extended to the full sentence/passage from the source text on file; 803 marked as fragments; 825 before the 2026-11-17 fragment fill). Rebuilt 2026-11-17 from 2968 old entries; old file: `characters/_bak/darth-krayt.md.bak-20261117`.
- **How to read:** each entry is the quotation in full (block quote), then its source and point of view. "Note" lines are summaries from our notes, not quotations. `[fragment — full passage not on file]` means the source text is not on the box, so only the words in our notes are shown. Debate and forum material is in the last section and is unverified.

# LEGENDS (Legends / EU — main focus)

## Legacy of the Force 1–9: novel notes from the other agent (reference copy)

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Nothing direct. Lumiya's dismissal of Palpatine (S15) is ideology. For existing comparisons, see `debates-sidious-plagueis-tenebrous-vs-krayt-caedus-valkorion.md`.
  — Source: Legacy of the Force 2: Bloodlines (novel), feats and scaling notes.
Our notes: `notes/materials/021-legacy-of-the-force/lotf-2-bloodlines-notes.md:166`

What it shows (our notes): ~30 cloaked Sith on balconies (L3917, L3925); leader "White Eyes" (pale white eyes, L3919). «You were taught the old ways ... We are only one Sith now.» (L3923) — the One Sith (cf. Krayt's order in Legacy era; Krayt not named here). Datachip torn from her hand by TK (L3929).
> “You were taught the old ways,” the voice said. “We are only one Sith now.”
>
> Alema had counted more than thirty, but it did not serve her purpose to call the man on his obvious lie. Despite what she had told Morto, her purpose here was not to learn about the Sith Order—though that would obviously prove useful. She only needed to win its help for Jacen. She reached inside her cloak for Lumiya’s datachip—then lifted her brow when the gesture caused thirty lightsabers to ignite in the blink of an eye.
— Source: L3923; L3929 — Legacy of the Force 6: Inferno (novel, read in full): feats, scaling and story notes.

## The Complete Star Wars Encyclopedia, Vol. I (A–G)

> Andara A wealthy, influential world within the Andara system. Another planet in the sys­ tem, Ieria, was dissatisfied by Andaran repre­ sentation in the Senate and battled for its own representation. Andara was the site of the Leadership School, an elite private academy. Andeddu, Darth A long-dead Sith Lord whose holocron was sought by Quinlan Vos and Skorr on Korriban in order to curry favor with Count Dooku. Hidden within the holo­ cron was the red crystal that powered An­ deddu's lightsaber. Dooku gave this crystal to Quinlan Vos. Over a century later, during the time of Roan Fel's empire, Darth Krayt was in possession of Andeddu's holocron. Ando A watery planet in the Mid Rim with few solid landmasses, Ando was home to the belligerent, walrus-faced Aqualish. The higher-class Aquala ("webbed" Aqualish) pre­ ferred to live on floating raft cities and large sailing ships, while the low-status Quara ("fin- Ancient Order of Pessimists gered" Aqualish) inhabited the larger islands of Ando. Ando mainly exported foodstuffs from its seas. A sister planet in the same system had a blasted, uninhabitable surface, the appar­ ent aftermath of an Aqualish war soon after the species discovered space travel.
— Source: scan pages 41–80; Darth Andeddu; Roan Fel; Darth Krayt — entry/section: Andeddu, Darth.

> A select few Sith Lords are given the title of Emperor's Hand, a tradition that Darth Krayt has resurrected from the days of Palpatine. These Hands function as the Emperor's agents throughout the galaxy, performing secret tasks at Krayt's whim. The position of Emperor's Hand is one of great honor, and it is fought over among the Sith Lords viciously. Many of the Sith struggle to prove their worthiness to Darth Krayt in hopes of becoming the next Hand, both to become one of the Emperor's most trusted agents but also to get close enough to him to be in position should the Emperor stumble and the chance to strike him down arise. Rumors exist that Krayt has at least one non-Sith Hand, though none of the Sith Lords have ever seen or heard from such an individual.
— Source: Sourcebooks/Legacy Era Campaign Guide.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Sag; Page contents: Sidebar The Emperor's Hands.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Page contents: Sidebar The Emperor's Hands: "A select few Sith Lords are given the title of Emperor's Hand, a tradition that Darth Krayt has resurrected from the days of Palpatine. These Hands function as the Emperor's agents throughout the galaxy, performing secret tasks at Krayt's whim. The position of Emperor's Hand is one of great honor, and it is fought over among the Sith Lords viciously. Many of the Sith struggle to prove their worthiness to Darth Krayt in hopes of becoming the next Hand, both to become one of the Emperor's most trusted agents but also to get close enough to him to be in position should the Emperor stumble and the chance to strike him down arise. Rumors exist that Krayt has at least one non-Sith Hand, though none of the Sith Lords have ever seen or heard from such an individual." The Council of Moffs (six members: three civilian, three military); Regional Governors & Sector Government; Moffs. (hand-read)
Our notes: `notes/materials/037-sw-d20-folder/719-legacy-era-campaign-guide-scan-121-180.md:295`; `suspect-insight/debates/081-cade-skywalker-vs-darth-nox.md:86`

> One of the most appealing aspects of the Legacy era is the fact that items from the past have just as much of a place in the present as they did in bygone eras. Nowhere is this more true than in the case of the Empire. Almost any weapons, piece of technology, character archetype, or concept that held true in the Dark Times, Rebellion era, or New Republic era is perfectly acceptable for use in the Legacy era. For example, the Galactic Empire under Palpatine had dozens of different types of stormtroopers, including RadTroopers, Imperial Storm Commandos, Jumptroopers, and so forth. The Empire under Darth Krayt has all of these things as well, and more. Gamemasters might need to adapt some concepts to the realities of the Legacy era, but for the most part anything that has existed in the past for the Empire can exist for the Empire in the Legacy era. *(hand-read)*
— Source: Sourcebooks/Legacy Era Campaign Guide.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Sag.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Page contents: Independent Affiliates (Hapes Consortium, Chiss Ascendancy); The Sector Rangers; The New Nobility (Bothawui); Sidebar Imperial Legacy: "One of the most appealing aspects of the Legacy era is the fact that items from the past have just as much of a place in the present as they did in bygone eras. Nowhere is this more true than in the case of the Empire. Almost any weapons, piece of technology, character archetype, or concept that held true in the Dark Times, Rebellion era, or New Republic era is perfectly acceptable for use in the Legacy era. For example, the Galactic Empire under Palpatine had dozens of different types of stormtroopers, including RadTroopers, Imperial Storm Commandos, Jumptroopers, and so forth. The Empire under Darth Krayt has all of these things as well, and more. Gamemasters might need to adapt some concepts to the realities of the Legacy era, but for the most part anything that has existed in the past for the Empire can exist for the Empire in the Legacy era." (hand-read)

## Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan pages 61–120)

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Page contents: Jedi Counseling 101 cont.: Our Alternative: Hit Points; quoted Saga Edition rules text: "Hit points (sometimes abbreviated "hp") represent two things in the game world: the ability to take physical punishment and keep going, and the ability to turn a serious blow into a graze or near miss. As you become more experienced, you become more adept at parrying strikes, dodging attacks, and rolling with blows such that you minimize or avoid significant physical trauma, but all this effort slowly wears you down. Rather than trying to keep track of the difference between attacks and how much physical injury you take, hit points are an abstract measure of your total ability to survive damage."; abstraction across character, Krayt dragon, tank, wall; 100-hp soldier vs. 10-hp stormtrooper ("virtual hit points"); Damage Threshold and Condition Track (penalties -1, -2, -5, -10; five steps = unconscious/disabled; catch breath; 0 hp below threshold = unconscious, above = possibly killed; threshold rises with level, Jedi Master vs. Sith Lord). (hand-read)
  — Source: Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan p.
Our notes: `notes/materials/037-sw-d20-folder/732-web-articles-scan-061-120.md:85`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Page contents: Jedi Counseling 103: Defense, Offense and Mini Names: Reflex, Fortitude and Will Defenses replace Defense and saving throws (save +5 vs DC 15 = attack +5 vs DC 16); frag grenade at six stormtroopers resolved with one attack roll, no scatter, misses take half damage; skill checks vs. Defenses (Force slam vs Fortitude, Weaken Resolve vs Will, Deception, Persuasion, Use Computer vs a computer's Will Defense); Defense = 10 + heroic level (or armor bonus) + class bonus + ability modifier + misc; class bonuses don't stack (10th-level soldier R21/F22/W20 vs soldier 5/scout 5 R22/F22/W20); damage threshold = Fortitude Defense, Improved Damage Threshold +5, size bonuses (Krayt dragon), armor Fortitude bonus; offense: heroes not just Jedi are better than fodder, all heroes add 1/2 heroic level to damage. (hand-read)
  — Source: Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan p.
Our notes: `notes/materials/037-sw-d20-folder/732-web-articles-scan-061-120.md:113`

Our notes: `notes/materials/037-sw-d20-folder/732-web-articles-scan-301-360.md:165`

> He created a new Sith order by abandoning the centuries-old Rule of Two. His seizure of the Imperial throne was not a complete success; the previous Emperor survived the takeover, as did a number of Jedi Knights. However, Darth Krayt is far older than anyone suspects, and he suffers from an insidious ailment that threatens to consume him. He's in dire need of powerful healing. As such, he's obsessed with pursuing and turning Cade Skywalker to the dark side because the young man possesses a unique gift that might heal the Dark Lord. In the Star Wars Miniatures Game, Darth Krayt is a formidable unit. His scores are higher than most of the Sith faction, and his Vonduun Crab Armor helps mitigate any damage he sustains. As in the comics, Darth Krayt is more effective in combat when allies are close at hand. His commander effect significantly increases the offensive capabilities of his followers when they are within 6 squares, at the cost of some overall squad maneuverability.
— Source: Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan p; Darth Krayt. POV: Darth Krayt.

> Luke could turn Vader only because of the ties between them. We've seen just how merciless and dangerous Krayt can get when mad. Cade understands that, so long as Krayt lives, the Sith Order will be intact and Cade will never know peace. Cade is not concerned about redeeming Krayt or about saving the galaxy, when you come right down to it. He wants people to leave him alone. He wants to live his life on his own terms, not anyone else's. Krayt needs to die. Now, whether or not he can do that is an entirely different question. Cade's gotten himself in over his head before. We'll see if it happens again.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #101 (2008).

## Star Wars: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels

> NO, YOU USE THE FORCE TO PURGE YOURSELF… LIKE A SITH…
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> THEN HE REALIZES THE LIMITATIONS OF THE JEDI TEACHINGS.
>
> I AM HERE ON ORDERS FROM OUR LORD KRAYT. SKYWALKER IS WANTED ALIVE.
>
> …HE'S GOT DELUSIONS OF ADEQUACY.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> AND IF KRAYT DOES DIE, WHO WILL LEAD THE SITH ORDER?
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Corrections, 2026-10-21 (from the page-by-page re-read of *Legacy Vol. 3: Claws of the Dragon*, owner material 013, where 013 pN = 009 p(N+301); each was checked on zoomed crops): (1) p436: Deliah does not fly the *Mynock* or catch Cade; Chak is flying, Deliah is on the guns, and Syn's hand catches Cade at the hatch. (2) p338: the torturer is Darth Maladi, not Talon; "NO, YOU USE THE FORCE TO PURGE YOURSELF… LIKE A SITH…" and "I SUPPLY THEM." are Maladi's, and "THEN HE REALIZES THE LIMITATIONS OF THE JEDI TEACHINGS." is the arriving Krayt; the crop was renamed `p338-maladi-cade-purges-drug-like-sith.jpg`. (3) p331: "I AM HERE ON ORDERS FROM OUR LORD KRAYT. SKYWALKER IS WANTED ALIVE." is Talon, not Nihl. (4) p341: "…HE'S GOT DELUSIONS OF ADEQUACY." is Syn, not Deliah. (5) p377: "AND IF KRAYT DOES DIE, WHO WILL LEAD THE SITH ORDER?" is Maladi and "NO HEIR HAS BEEN NAMED…" is Nihl. (6) p382: "SKYWALKER'S ARROGANCE WILL BE HIS UNDOING…" is Krayt, not Wyyrlok. (7) p413: "THEN TAKE THIS SABER AND STRIKE ME DOWN!…" names no addressee; Cade holds the offered saber, so "Krayt to Hosk" is changed to addressee UNCLEAR, most likely Cade. (8) p417 plot wording: the Sith recoil from Cade's burst; they aren't scattered or knocked down. See `013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md`, "Differences vs 009".
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:12`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Roan Fel confronts Moff Nyna Calixte: the Sith framed the Vong, and she and Veed helped plan it (p149–152). Fel ordered that the Jedi be allowed to withdraw to Ossus (p154). After the massacre, Maladi tells Calixte that Krayt will take the throne (p155–156). Calixte warns Fel. An Imperial Knight Force-chokes and throws her, and she claims she can shield her thoughts from Fel (p160–162). Mohrgan and the Knights decoy for Fel's double; Krayt kills them all in a wordless sequence (p163–166). Fel feels them die and escapes (p167). Maladi makes Calixte report to her (p169–170).

> ALREADY DEAD. I KILLED HER AS SOON AS THE BOY RAN OFF. *(p245–249; corrected 2026-10-21)*
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Darth Krul (lettered "KRUHL" in dialogue) kills clan chief Rikkar-Du, his son and their dog, while the elder Kassek-Ka kills Rikkar's wife Tassa: "ALREADY DEAD. I KILLED HER AS SOON AS THE BOY RAN OFF." (p245–249; corrected 2026-10-21). Maladi kills Governor Dorn (p261). He infiltrates Bastion and duels Roan Fel. Fel jams Krul's lightsaber with a cortosis gauntlet, catches his Force lightning on his own saber and turns it back, fights him hand to hand, and shoots him dead (p254–260). Maladi kills Dorn, and Krayt tells her her stratagem failed (p261–262).
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:65`

Sazen senses this from Ossus (p332).
  — Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.

> all the skills and abilities of a Jedi *(p458)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> I'VE WANTED TO TEST MYSELF AGAINST THE SITH! THEY'LL HAVE LOST THE ELEMENT OF SURPRISE *(p162)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): Krayt's best on-panel melee feat in the book. He solos a group of fully trained Force-users. The handbook says Imperial Knights have "all the skills and abilities of a Jedi" (p458). Mohrgan said beforehand: "I'VE WANTED TO TEST MYSELF AGAINST THE SITH! THEY'LL HAVE LOST THE ELEMENT OF SURPRISE" (p162), so the Knights were expecting him. Caveat: we don't know their individual tier, and Krayt is visibly drained afterwards (entry 5).

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Scaling (ANALYSIS): a holocron vision shows his fear (the Vong spikes consuming him), and he banishes it with lightning. That's a mental/willpower framing feat. Bane's "PRETENDER" line is in-universe disdain, not a power ranking. Debaters on the Bane/Krayt or Nihilus/Krayt sides can each cite it; it shows only that the holocrons (gatekeepers' recordings) refused to teach him.

> 17 BBY Ben needed full focus vs Jedi A'Sharad Hett
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): the core Kenobi link in the Krayt chains (verdicts.md #2 and #6 use "17 BBY Ben needed full focus vs Jedi A'Sharad Hett"). On these pages the duel runs four mostly wordless pages (p366–369). Hett has two sabers plus the gaderffii and lands a kick ("OOF!"), and they trade Force pushes. Then Kenobi takes his hand off and strips his weapons, and the duel ends decisively. Nothing on these pages says Kenobi "needed full focus"; that wording comes from elsewhere (likely the issue's prose or a guide). As drawn, the panels show Kenobi > Hett, clearly. Krayt himself calls it "MY HUMILIATION BY OBI-WAN KENOBI" (p373).
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:240`

> holds off Darth Krayt in a prolonged lightsaber duel, taxing Krayt into stasis
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> battle with Skywalker has cost me
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: (debate #81) — Owner Material 009 — Star Wars: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): Krayt is never in danger from Cade. The exchange is one or two blocks, a taunt and a rage strike, then a blaster hit from outside and Cade jumps out of the window. Krayt is also explicitly holding back, since he needs Cade alive (p146, p346, p413: "YOU TOLD ME YOU NEED ME TO HEAL YOU"). legends-feats.md says (debate #81) Cade "holds off Darth Krayt in a prolonged lightsaber duel, taxing Krayt into stasis". Book 1 doesn't show that. In Claws the duel is brief, and the "battle with Skywalker has cost me" line is Legacy #27 (outside this book). Cade does perceive Krayt's illness ("WHAT FIGHTING ME IS COSTING YOU"), which a Krayt-side debater would call a sickness caveat, not a measure of Cade's power.
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:331`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Scaling (ANALYSIS): across the book, Talon vs Cade comes out roughly even. She wins the ambush (p411) and in training (p381), the first temple fight is a draw interrupted by Nihl, and he wins the cuffed surprise blast (p372) and the p418 strike. The framing makes her his superior in skill, while he out-classes her in raw power.
  — Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:648`

> SO. YOU TOO SURVIVED ORDER 66. I THOUGHT I WAS ALONE. *(p364)*
>
> Clipping his lightsaber to his belt, Ben said, “The Tuskens were once your people, but so were the Jedi. You have forgotten our ways. Perhaps, with meditation, you will remember them and yourself.”
>
> I HOPE YOU WILL. MAY THE FORCE BE WITH YOU, A'SHARAD HETT. *(p370; both checked against the page image)*
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Star Wars®: The Life and Legend of Obi-Wan Kenobi (Ryder Windham) (text copy `MEGA_Other_Juvenile_Books_Star_Wars_The_Life_and_Legend_of_Obi_Wan_Kenobi_Ryder_Windham_epub`).)
Note (our notes, around the quote): Beats A'Sharad Hett and spares him (see Krayt 15, p363 (#16 Claws)–p370 (#16 Claws)). Hett: "SO. YOU TOO SURVIVED ORDER 66. I THOUGHT I WAS ALONE." (p364). Kenobi, after the duel: "THE TUSKENS WERE ONCE YOUR PEOPLE, BUT SO WERE THE JEDI. YOU HAVE FORGOTTEN OUR WAYS. PERHAPS, WITH MEDITATION, YOU WILL REMEMBER THEM AND YOURSELF." · "I HOPE YOU WILL. MAY THE FORCE BE WITH YOU, A'SHARAD HETT." (p370; both checked against the page image).
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:1017`

> Twenty-five years after Luke Skywalker and the Rebel Alliance freed the galaxy from the tyranny of Emperor Palpatine and Darth Vader, a new threat emerged: alien invaders called Yuuzhan Vong.
>
> But, unknown to all, a new Sith Order was secretly rising on the planet Korriban.
>
> Sabotaging the Vong's transformation processes, the Sith used the disastrous results to incite war between the Empire and the Galactic Alliance. Emperor Roan Fel tried to end the war, but his own Moffs were secretly allied with the Sith and thwarted his efforts.
>
> Following the disastrous Battle of Caamas, the Galactic Alliance was defeated. Many Jedi were subsequently killed in the Massacre of Ossus and the rest scattered as fugitives throughout the galaxy. Within days of the defeat, the Sith also moved against Emperor Fel. He, however, escaped into exile, and the Moff Council's supposed victory was turned into defeat as the Sith's leader, Darth Krayt, proclaimed himself emperor . . .
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels; The State of the Galaxy (p445–446).
![p445 Handbook: The State of the Galaxy](../../notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1-panels/p445-handbook-state-of-the-galaxy.jpg) *(p445)*
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:1118`

> Ancient and deadly, Darth Krayt spent nearly a century secretly building a new Sith Order on Korriban. The Rule of Two is gone, banished by the new Sith leader. Now there is only the One—the Sith Order itself.
>
> Known only to a few is the fact that Krayt was once known as A'Sharrad Hett [sic], a Jedi who had survived the Clone Wars and who had become embittered about the fate of the galaxy. Hett discovered—and learned from—an ancient Sith holocron on the planet Korriban; was captured, tortured, and transformed by the Yuuzhan Vong; and finally emerged as Darth Krayt. A combination of stasis chambers and Sith meditation has allowed him to artificially extend his life, but even Krayt cannot live forever.
>
> Krayt seeks someone who can heal him of the Yuuzhan Vong infection which is slowly overtaking his body. But thus far his efforts to rid himself of the Vong parasites have failed.
>
> Darth Krayt's legion of Sith now outnumber the Jedi. *(The art shows Krayt with Force lightning crackling from his hands.)*
— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels; Darth Krayt (p448).

> (p2) "I REMEMBER SEARING PAIN, EVERY NERVE FIBER ABLAZE...THEN DARKNESS AND COLD. I REMEMBER THE YAWNING ABYSS OF THE GRAVE WAITING TO SUCK ME ALL THE WAY IN." · "I REMEMBER A SECRET I LEARNED THAT OPENED THE GALAXY TO ME ANEW, AND MADE ME SEE IT DIFFERENTLY. MY BETRAYER WILL KNOW MY REVENGE. THEN, I WILL BEND THE GALAXY TO MY WILL."

> (p23) "ONCE, I THOUGHT TO BRING PEACE AND ORDER TO THE GALAXY THROUGH THE EXISTING GOVERNMENTS -- TO ALIGN THE WORLDS TO THE RULE OF THE ONE SITH." · "THAT CANNOT WORK. THE GALAXY MUST EXPERIENCE THE PAIN OF DEATH AND THE RAPTURE OF REBIRTH AS I HAVE." · "I WILL BRING CHAOS." · "IT IS TIME FOR WAR." · (p46, to Talon) "WHEN I CALLED TO THE ONE SITH THROUGH THE FORCE, I FELT SKYWALKER'S PRESENCE. AS MUCH AS THE BOY BELIEVES HE WALKS IN THE LIGHT, DARKNESS STILL SHROUDS HIM." · "FIND HIM AND MAKE HIM A GIFT OF YOUR BROKEN IMPERIAL KNIGHT." · (to Cade's holo) "DEATH IS NOT AN ENDING, BOY -- BUT IT IS A PASSAGEWAY TO SOMETHING GREATER. IT IS SOMETHING YOU, TOO, MUST EXPERIENCE, CADE SKYWALKER." · "FOR I HAVE HAD A VISION. YOU WILL BEND, YOU WILL BREAK, AND YOU WILL SERVE AT MY SIDE. BUT FIRST -- YOU MUST DIE." · (p60) "UNTIL THE FORCE SPEAKS TO YOU, DO NOT QUESTION MY JUDGMENT, GRAND ADMIRAL." · (to Nihl) "NONE. THEY ARE MERELY BAIT. I HAVE FORESEEN HOW THIS ALLIANCE WILL REACT TO THE IMMINENT ATTACK. THIS IS THE FIRST STEP IN MY FINAL PLAN TO BRING THE GALAXY TO HEEL."

## Star Wars Saga Edition: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Page references: "p" means the PDF page. Printed book page = PDF page − 1 (the printed contents on p4 show "3"; p50 prints "49"). The book's own cross-references and the appendix use printed numbers. For example, the appendix lists Krayt at "168", which is PDF p169–170.
  — Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide.md:9`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Darth Krayt and Emperor Roan Fel are the only CL 20 characters in the book, and both have Use the Force +22 (p170, p178; appendix p224). Krayt has better defences and attack (Ref 36/Fort 38/Will 38; lightsaber +24, 2d8+12 ×3 crit). Fel has more hit points (157 vs 135).
  — Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p170 Krayt bio: implants "begin to consume him"; Cade's "incredible healing powers"](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-krayt-bio-dying-cade-healing.jpg)
![p170 Darth Krayt CL 20 stat block](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-darth-krayt-stat-cl20.jpg)
![p170 art: "Darth Krayt and Darth Wyyrlok"](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-art-krayt-and-wyyrlok.jpg)
![p178 Roan Fel bio](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p178-roan-fel-bio.jpg)
![p178 Emperor Roan Fel CL 20](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p178-emperor-roan-fel-stat-cl20.jpg)
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p178 — full quote as printed](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/quotes/cg-p178-the-jedi-have-nothing-to-fear.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide.md:30`

> Arguably the most powerful man in the galaxy *(p169)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> The Yuuzhan Vong implants used to extend his life begin to consume him. Furthermore, healing trances and Force healing become less effective *(p170)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Krayt's prose framing is maximal, but his body is failing.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Krayt's prose framing is maximal, but his body is failing: "Arguably the most powerful man in the galaxy" (p169), and "The Yuuzhan Vong implants used to extend his life begin to consume him. Furthermore, healing trances and Force healing become less effective" (p170). A sidebar states flatly that he "is dying" (p54). His Con is 10, so his hp (135) is lower than Nihl's (145), Azard's and Draco's (137) and Fel's (157).
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p169 — full quote as printed](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/quotes/cg-p169-arguably-the-most-powerful-man-in.jpg)
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p170 — full quote as printed (panel)](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-krayt-bio-dying-cade-healing.jpg)
![p54 sidebar: Darth Krayt "is dying"](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p054-krayt-holocron-dying.jpg)
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p54 — full quote as printed (panel)](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p054-dark-transfer.jpg)
![p169 Krayt bio: "Arguably the most powerful man in the galaxy"](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p169-krayt-bio-most-powerful-man.jpg)
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p169 — full quote as printed](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/quotes/cg-p169-arguably-the-most-powerful-man-in-2.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide.md:31`

> one of the most formidable lightsaber duelists in the Sith Order *(p173–174, p163)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Sith rank order by the numbers: Krayt CL20 (UtF +22) > Wyyrlok III CL16 (UtF +20) ≈ Nihl CL16 (UtF +15, but the best Sith duelist line after Krayt: +21, 2d10+10) > Azard CL15 / Maladi CL15 > Talon CL13 / Maleval CL13. Talon is statted barely above the generic Imperial Knight (CL11), despite the bio calling her "one of the most formidable lightsaber duelists in the Sith Order" (p173–174, p163).

> are immune to any Force effect that targets Will Defense (including Force powers and aspects of the Use the Force skill) *(p17, p34–35)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Yuuzhan Vong Force immunity is hard-coded: Vong.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Yuuzhan Vong Force immunity is hard-coded: Vong "are immune to any Force effect that targets Will Defense (including Force powers and aspects of the Use the Force skill)" (p17, p34–35). Their bio-implants cost Force-users −1 UtF each, up to −5 (p68). Krayt carries two such implants (p170).
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p17 — full quote as printed (panel)](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p017-vong-force-immunity.jpg)

> He rescinds the Rule of Two put in place by Darth Bane… He issues the Rule of One: the declaration that there will be one Sith Order composed of many Sith Lords and apprentices, all united under his rule *(p53)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Krayt is the supreme ruler of the Empire, and his word is law *(p157)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Krayt's Sith Lords operate in the open, unlike the secret apprentices and dark Force-users that served under Palpatine and Darth Vader *(p157)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> fought over among the Sith Lords viciously
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> get close enough to him to be in position should the Emperor stumble and the chance to strike him down arise. Rumors exist that Krayt has at least one non-Sith Hand, though none of the Sith Lords have ever seen or heard from such an individual.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Krayt's Galactic Empire (One Sith plus the Moff Council)..
Note (our notes, around the quote): Krayt's Galactic Empire (One Sith plus the Moff Council). "He rescinds the Rule of Two put in place by Darth Bane… He issues the Rule of One: the declaration that there will be one Sith Order composed of many Sith Lords and apprentices, all united under his rule" (p53). "Krayt is the supreme ruler of the Empire, and his word is law" (p157). "Krayt's Sith Lords operate in the open, unlike the secret apprentices and dark Force-users that served under Palpatine and Darth Vader" (p157). The Emperor's Hands sidebar (p158) says the title is "fought over among the Sith Lords viciously", partly to "get close enough to him to be in position should the Emperor stumble and the chance to strike him down arise. Rumors exist that Krayt has at least one non-Sith Hand, though none of the Sith Lords have ever seen or heard from such an individual."
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p53 — full quote as printed (panel)](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p053-rule-of-one.jpg)

> Arguably the most powerful man in the galaxy, Darth Krayt is both the leader of the Sith and the reigning Emperor. Born and raised among Tusken Raiders on Tatooine, Darth Krayt's original name is A'Sharad Hett. Trained as a Jedi Knight, Hett manages to survive Order 66 by escaping his clone troopers and then slaying them.
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.

> [fragment — full passage not on file]

> By the end of the Sith-Imperial War, Darth Krayt declares himself Emperor. However, his body suffers the effects of having lived far beyond its natural lifespan. The Yuuzhan Vong implants used to extend his life begin to consume him. Furthermore, healing trances and Force healing become less effective. Darth Krayt knows that his time is limited unless a new solution is found. At one point he captures Cade Skywalker, an heir to the Skywalker bloodline gifted with incredible healing powers, in the hopes that one day he will be able to harness those same powers to prolong his life.
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Bio, continued (p170).
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p170 — full quote as printed (panel)](../../notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-krayt-bio-dying-cade-healing.jpg)

## Star Wars: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): 130 ABY (Allies, p10–31; and the p163–166 flashback): Roan Fel and his cousin Mohrgan with the Imperial Knights; Krayt's coup; Maladi as Krayt's agent; teen Cade adrift after Ossus, picked up by Rav's pirates.
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:36`

> HAD SOME SUCCESS IN SHIELDING MY THOUGHTS FROM FORCE USERS. YOU, FOR EXAMPLE. *(p21–23)*
— Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): On Bastion, Roan Fel accuses Moff Nyna Calixte: the Sith sabotaged the Vong terraforming and framed the Vong, and she and Veed helped plan it (p10–13). Fel orders that the Jedi be allowed to withdraw to Ossus (p15). Three hours after Ossus falls, Maladi tells Veed and Calixte that Krayt wants Fel replaced (p16–17). Calixte warns Fel that the Sith will assassinate him at the Moff Council. A Knight Force-chokes and throws her. She says she has "HAD SOME SUCCESS IN SHIELDING MY THOUGHTS FROM FORCE USERS. YOU, FOR EXAMPLE." (p21–23). Mohrgan leads the Knights escorting Fel's double into the throne room. Krayt asks the "Emperor" for "YOUR LIFE" (p25) and, in a wordless page, cuts down the Knight squad alone, running Mohrgan through (p27). In the tunnels, Fel feels them die (p28). Krayt kills the double and takes the throne (p29). Maladi makes Calixte report to her and orders her to find Fel (p30–31).
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:44`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): #9: Maladi breaks the Jedi healer Hosk Trey'lis with interrogator droids and her will (p58–60). Krayt wants Cade "ALIVE"; Maladi doesn't know why (p61). Calixte tells Veed that Maladi's top agent is "JOR TOLIN" (sic; Torlin elsewhere), Sith-raised but lacking "SUFFICIENT FORCE SENSITIVITY", and reactivates "Morrigan Corde" (p62). She spots Cade on the Wheel's security vids (p63–64). On Bastion, Fel punishes Draco and Krieg for their rescue mission with medals and the most dangerous assignments, and sends them to escort Capt. Bovark to GA Admiral Gar Stazi on the Wheel (p65–67). Talks begin (p68–70). The two Knights sense the GA guards' concealed blasters (p71). On the Wheel's lower levels, Torlin fights a gang that Corde set on him. Corde joins in, Torlin strangles her, and she shocks him unconscious (p72–79).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p59 Maladi: "YOUR WILL IS NO MATCH FOR MINE"](../../notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p059-maladi-will-breaks-hosk.jpg)
![p62 "JOR TOLIN" dossier](../../notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p062-jor-tolin-dossier.jpg)
![p65 Fel: "WE PUT FIVE OF THEM ON HIS SHIP, KRIEG."](../../notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p065-fel-five-homing-devices.jpg)
![p71 the Knights on the Wheel](../../notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p071-knights-sense-ga-guards.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:52`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): On Munto Codru, clan chief Rikkar-Du tries to unite the Codru-Ji clans against the Empire after Darth Krul ("KRUHL") offers him rule (p104–107). Krul kills him, his son Jassar and their dog. Elder Kassek-Ka, now the Sith's man, killed Rikkar's wife Tassa (p108–112). Maladi uses Governor Vikar Dorn and the ISD *Dauntless* as a "defection" to get Krul onto Bastion (p113–116). Krul slips out and confronts Fel in the Pellaeon Gardens. Fel jams Krul's saber with a cortosis gauntlet, catches his lightning on his own blade and turns it back, brawls with him, and shoots him with a holdout blaster (p117–123). Fel has the *Dauntless* crew killed and sends Dorn back to Krayt with Krul's hilt. Maladi runs Dorn through (p124). Krayt warns her not to "SCHEME FOR SCHEMING'S SAKE" (p125).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:57`

> (p25) Captions/dialogue: "THE EMPIRE OWES YOU MUCH. OUR VICTORY WOULD NOT BE POSSIBLE WITHOUT YOU. WHAT REWARD WOULD YOU NAME?" · Krayt: "YOUR LIFE." · Mohrgan: "IMPERIAL KNIGHTS! SAVE YOUR EMPEROR!" · (p23, before) Mohrgan: "I'VE WANTED TO TEST MYSELF AGAINST THE SITH! THEY'LL HAVE LOST THE ELEMENT OF SURPRISE."
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Flashbacks, narrated by Krayt or Talon (framing by an interested narrator): Jedi General A'Sharad Hett at Order 66 (p61); Hett as Tusken warlord vs Obi-Wan Kenobi on Tatooine while Luke is hidden on the Lars farm (p62–70); Hett as bounty hunter, then XoXaan's apprentice on Korriban (p89–91, p98); Hett's capture and shaping by the Yuuzhan Vong and his meetings with Vergere (p99–104); the founding of Krayt's Order, Lumiya and "CADEUS", stasis (p105–106).

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): From stasis, Krayt senses "SKYWALKER IS COMING" while Wyyrlok fears he is dying (p10). Cade shakes off a four-ship Skull Squadron flight in Coruscant traffic and hides his ship in the undercity (p11–14, p22). He leaves the *Mynock*'s codes with Queen Jool at Rik's cantina for Syn and Deliah (p17–21). Gunner Yage is dressed down by her father (p22–23). Cade slips into the Sith Temple, finds Hosk with the Force and frees him (p24–27). Talon ambushes them; Cade has the better of the first exchange until Nihl strikes him from behind (p27–30). Sazen senses Cade's darkness from Ossus (p31).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p10 Wyyrlok: "MY LORD IS DYING"; Krayt: "SKYWALKER IS COMING."](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p010-wyyrlok-my-lord-is-dying.jpg)
![p22 "AN ACE PILOT TO DUST ALL FOUR OF US"](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p022-skull-dust-all-four-of-us.jpg)

Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:40`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Maladi calls off the Imperial search; the Moffs squabble, and Calixte, Veed and the Yages trade barbs (p32–36). Maladi tortures Cade with ixetal cilona, which he keeps purging; Krayt arrives (p37–38). Talon brings in Syn and Deliah; Maladi triggers coral seeds in them, Krayt frees one of Cade's hands with the Force, and Cade heals both at once with dark-side power (p39–45). Cade trades his freedom for theirs (p45–46). Calixte and Veed plan to use Corde (p47–48). Wyyrlok keeps Nihl and Maladi outside; they argue over birth and rank (p49–50). Over dinner Krayt shows Cade Kol's encased lightsaber and reveals he was the Jedi A'Sharad Hett (p51–53).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:44`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Corde gets past Veed's security, demands three dozen Vong bugs and a stand-down "ON MY SIGNAL" (p54). At Rik's she recruits Syn, Deliah, Chak and Kee and tells them she is Cade's mother (p55–59). Krayt tells his story: Clone Wars, Order 66, the Tuskens, and his defeat and maiming by Kenobi (p60–69). Cade mocks him, Talon throws him with the Force, and Cade blasts her across the room and threatens to "EXPLODE" her weak point; Krayt stops him, cuts his cuffs, orders him to heal her and makes Talon his teacher; Cade asks to be taught "THE WAYS OF THE SITH" (p70–73). On Ossus the Jedi hide from Stryfe's troopers (p74–75).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:48`

> THAT WHICH CAN HEAL CAN ALSO BREAK. *(p115)*
— Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Cade hangs in the Vong "EMBRACE OF PAIN" while Krayt tells the rest: Vong capture, shaping, Vergere, his vision, the escape, the new Order, Lumiya and "CADEUS", stasis, and his dying body (p98–107). Cade slips out of his cell; the crew launches (p108–109). Talon is waiting in Maladi's lab and drops him (p110). Krayt orders him to kill Hosk; Cade refuses, Hosk forgives everyone, and Krayt runs Hosk through; Hosk's body vanishes (p111–114). Kol's ghost: "THAT WHICH CAN HEAL CAN ALSO BREAK." (p115). Cade's telekinetic burst shatters the case, he calls Kol's saber, strikes Talon and engages Nihl while Krayt urges revenge (p116–119).
![p110 Talon repels Cade: "IDIOT."](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p110-talon-repels-cade-idiot.jpg)
![p111 "DID YOU THINK YOU COULD HIDE..."](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p111-krayt-saw-through-pretense.jpg)

![p113 Krayt runs Hosk through](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p113-krayt-kills-hosk.jpg)
![p114 Hosk's body vanishes; Krayt: "IT'S A LIE!"](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p114-hosk-vanishes-no-one-dies.jpg)
![p115 Kol's ghost: "THAT WHICH CAN HEAL CAN ALSO BREAK."](../../notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p115-kol-ghost-heal-can-break.jpg)

> BECAUSE YOU WANT ME TO!
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> I WILL BREAK HIM AND I WILL USE HIM AND THEN I WILL KILL HIM. *(p137)*
— Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Corde rocketpacks up the Temple; Syn's bugs blow the main hangar (p120–122), the "signal" Veed recognises (p123). Cade beats Nihl but refuses to kill him "BECAUSE YOU WANT ME TO!" and attacks Krayt (p124–126). Skull Squadron is ordered off (p127, p130–131). Krayt boasts and Cade taunts him about the Vong growths (p128–129); Krayt has Cade on the floor when Corde shoots him through the window (p132). Cade jumps from the tower and Syn catches him in the *Mynock* (p133–135). Corde tells Cade she is his mother and leaves by the undercity (p136). Krayt: "I WILL BREAK HIM AND I WILL USE HIM AND THEN I WILL KILL HIM." (p137). Veed and Calixte discuss the outcome; alone, Calixte plays a hologram of Kol, herself and toddler Cade: "RUN." (p138–141).
Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:60`

Four Sith (Nihl, a spiked-armoured figure with a raised red saber who is Wyyrlok by count but faceless, Talon, and Krayt) recoil and shield themselves; none is clearly knocked off their feet. On p117 Cade strikes Talon at close range: a green flare at her side while she grips his head. The hilt isn't clearly visible, so saber cut vs palm burst is UNCLEAR. She is down in the background for the rest of the fight (p128).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.

She wins in training (p78–80) and the forewarned ambush (p110); he wins the first exchange in the Temple (p29, before Nihl intervenes), the unguarded cuffed blast (p71) and the surprise close-range strike (p117). The framing makes her the more skilled and him the stronger.

## https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth/Legends

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Darth Krayt drove his fingers into Abeloth's stomach and drained her power. Krayt, Luke, and Abeloth became entangled in a Force-energy struggle; the article says Abeloth was weakened faster than Luke while Krayt drained her, but the drain also harmed Krayt and Luke [1].
  — Source: Abeloth research extraction (Wookieepedia / Star Wars Legends) — https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth/Legends.
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/abeloth-quotes.txt:54`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Abeloth tore away her energy bond with Luke and tried to bite them. Luke merged his form with hers again and urged Krayt to pull harder. Krayt recalled a severed hand lodged in Abeloth; Abeloth then tore at both men and left each with a fist-sized hole in the chest while she escaped with some of their Force essence [1].
  — Source: Abeloth research extraction (Wookieepedia / Star Wars Legends) — https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth/Legends.
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/abeloth-quotes.txt:55`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): In the astral realm, Abeloth's Force lightning passed through Darth Krayt and hit Luke before Krayt could attack; it blasted Krayt backward [1].
  — Source: Abeloth research extraction (Wookieepedia / Star Wars Legends) — https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth/Legends.
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/abeloth-quotes.txt:59`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): The article says Krayt left Luke after stating that Sith dominance had only been delayed by Caedus and Abeloth. This is Krayt's statement about the future, not a feat [1].
  — Source: Abeloth research extraction (Wookieepedia / Star Wars Legends) — https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth ; https://starwars.fandom.com/wiki/Abeloth/Legends.
Our notes: `external/mega-notes/abeloth-quotes.txt:61`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): The article says Abeloth was not invincible: manifestations could be annihilated by a thermal detonator, and blades, lightsabers, blasters, or Force-thrown objects could wound her if they bypassed her guard [1][4][11]. Krayt's life-force drain harmed her and also harmed him and Luke [1].

> It was the man with the tattooed face—the one who had been behind Luke’s team in the Manarai Heights Spaceport, and who had later disarmed Yaqeel Saav’etu near Fellowship Plaza. “Who are you?”
>
> “No one whose help you want,” Jacen said. “That’s the dark man I saw on the Throne of Balance.”
>
> “And the only one who can help you,” the stranger said. “With the Ones gone, there is only one way to stop Abeloth …
>
> “And the only one who can help you,” the stranger said. “With the Ones gone, there is only one way to stop Abeloth … Jedi and Sith together.”
>
> Luke studied the stranger without answering for a moment, trying to imagine him without the scowl. The man was hardly ugly, but he certainly did not share the Lost Tribe’s usual obsession with careful grooming and good looks.

What it shows (our notes): FotJ: Apocalypse (quoted by ILS
What it shows (our notes): FotJ Apocalypse (as read by ILS): fights Abeloth beyond shadows alongside Luke, driving his fingers into her stomach (debate #114)
> There were no pressure points or joint locks or choke holds, only his presence merging with hers, binding him to her in a writhing knot of energy.
>
> Tentacles began to lash at his face, probing for his nose and ears and mouth. A pair of gray tips shot into view, blurring and growing large. Luke closed both eyes and turned away, but not quickly enough. The right eye socket exploded in pain, and everything went dark on that side of his head.
>
> The tattooed stranger stepped in from the left, then slid to the front and drove his stiffened fingers deep into the pit of Abeloth’s stomach. A black spray erupted from the wound, and she writhed in pain as the stranger probed for something to grab.
>
> Abeloth loosed a Force blast, trying to drive the stranger off.
— Source: Legends Feats & Quotes (Star Wars Legends / EU).

What it shows (our notes): Fought A'Sharad Hett for Luke's sake (The Life and Legend of Obi-Wan Kenobi) (debate #8)
> Hett's hands dropped to his belt and the two lightsabers practically leapt into his gloved hands. He ignited both weapons at once, unleashing their identical green energy beams. He swung fast with the lightsaber in his right hand but Ben blocked it. The lightsabers sizzled loudly as they clashed.
>
> It was fortunate for Ben that he had continued his Jedi exercises on Tatooine, that he had not allowed his reflexes to become dull. He did not think about how long it had been since he had last used his lightsaber in combat. Nor did he consider that he was older than Hett by at least a decade, or Hett's considerable skills with his own weapons, and that the Tusken was far more experienced at fighting in the desert. Ben knew that any such thoughts would probably only get him killed.
>
> As prepared as Ben was for many things, he was not ready to die. Not yet. Not today. Hett brought his other lightsaber in at a sharp angle, forcing Ben to lurch back. Ben gripped his own weapon with both hands as he swung at Hett's legs, but Hett blocked the swipe. There was another loud sizzle as the blades dragged across each other.
>
> Ben gasped as Hett launched a powerful kick to his midriff. The kick knocked Ben off his feet, and as he fell back through the air, Hett hurled one of his lightsabers at Ben's body. Ben clung tight to his own lightsaber as he twisted his body in midair to avoid being struck by the spinning blade of Hett's weapon. The moment Hett's lightsaber whipped past Ben's head, Hett used the Force to retrieve it, drawing it back to his waiting left hand.
>
> As Hett caught the lightsaber, Ben rolled up from the ground and swung out again. Hett blocked the strike with his right lightsaber, then threw his left arm forward to smash his other lightsaber's handle into Ben's jaw. Ben ignored the painful jolt to his head and reflexively brought his blade up high, forcing Hett to block the blow with his right lightsaber and leaving his own midsection briefly exposed. Before Hett could strike with his other lightsaber, Ben kicked him hard in the stomach.
>
> Hett grunted, but he didn't go down. He lashed out again at Ben, kicking up sand as he moved in for the kill. Not one of the mounted Tuskens so much as flinched as they watched the duel, nor did they rally for their chief. They merely watched in silence, waiting for the outcome.
>
> Ben blocked each blow, but he wasn't doing it with ease. Hett was far more experienced at fighting on the sand and in the desert heat. Ben knew that his opponent would never surrender, let alone withdraw. As much as he hoped to avoid killing Hett, he also knew that they couldn't keep fighting indefinitely.
>
> But in the end, Ben knew he wasn't fighting for his own life. He was fighting for Luke's.
>
> Quickly raising his left hand, Ben used the Force to push out at Hett, shoving him back through the air as Ben's lightsaber swept up and through Hett's right arm. Hett shouted as his arm fell away from his body. As Hett stumbled back, Ben used the Force to tear Hett's other lightsaber from his left hand's grip. Both of Hett's lightsabers deactivated as they sailed past Ben and landed in the sand behind him.
>
> — Star Wars: The Life and Legend of Obi-Wan Kenobi (cited by Master Azronger *(April 2nd 2020, 3:58 pmMessage reputation : 100% in debate #8)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>

Note (our notes, around the quote): _Added by the material 013 pass on 2026-10-21. Nothing above this heading was changed. Every quote was read from the page images of the *Legacy Volume 3: Claws of the Dragon* TPB (Legacy #14–19; 146-page scan), with lettering kept in capitals. "p" is the TPB's PDF page; the matching omnibus (009) page is p+301 throughout the story pages (p8–141). Issue numbers are inferred from 22-page issues (#14 p10–31, #15 p32–53, #16 p54–75, #17 p76–97, #18 p98–119, #19 p120–141), so they are marked ≈. Only items not already in the 009 appendix are listed. Panels and full context: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md`. Corrections to the 009 appendix above (not edited there): (a) the 009 p338 purge scene's torturer is Darth Maladi, not Talon (Krayt's p339 line is still Krayt's); (b) Krayt's "STRIKE ME DOWN! … NOTHING LESS WILL EVEN MAKE ME PAUSE!" (009 p413 = 013 p112) names no addressee; Cade is holding the offered saber, so it is most likely aimed at Cade rather than Hosk; (c) in the Claws tower fight Krayt has Cade on the floor when Corde's shot hits him (p132). The 009 note is now corrected in the same way._

Note (our notes, around the quote): "Firestorm of raw emotion and cold rage" is Wyyrlok's sensing of Krayt's presence (a p15 caption). It is not a description of each attack. Ant attaches it to every phase (lightning, rocks, sabers) to imply each was an all-out effort; the text does not support that.
Posted by: not named in our notes (see thread/notes link).
Also cited as: Darth Krayt vs Darth Wyyrlok" (Suspect Insight Forums) — https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t4456-dissecting-legacy-2-darth-krayt-vs-darth-wyyrlok; A6 (line 52; speaker: see context).
Also noted as: A6 (line 52; speaker: see context) — “firestorm of raw emotion and cold rage”  _[context: …**What are the implications of Wyyrlok surviving, seemingly, Krayt's]_

> If not for Antares Draco stopping the virus, which Krayt did not foresee or anticipate, Fel would have unleashed the virus before Krayt had actually defeated Cade… if Krayt could readily defeat Cade, he would have. The fact he didn't reveals he couldn't.

Note (our notes, around the quote): Omega Red time pressure. The toxin kills "within a matter of breaths" (p108), and Sith are immune. It probably keys on dark-side energy, and Cade is still "on the edge of light and dark" (p123), so he might not survive it. Krayt therefore has to finish fast. "If not for Antares Draco stopping the virus, which Krayt did not foresee or anticipate, Fel would have unleashed the virus before Krayt had actually defeated Cade… if Krayt could readily defeat Cade, he would have. The fact he didn't reveals he couldn't."

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Overall: the arguments from Krayt's intentions are the weakest part. Omega Red is mostly a vision device, and the toxin was Krayt's own plan. The strongest parts are the method point (one panel ≠ the whole duel) and the bolded "SURRENDER" with Krayt's escalation through several techniques. A probability case built on "likely" cannot carry the full-power conclusion.
